## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e153ea","wall_time_seconds":0.233868,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":5879,"output":"---\\r\\ntitle: \\"Obsidian Plugin Atlas - Priority Report\\"\\r\\nsource: \\"obsidian-plugin-atlas-combined.html\\"\\r\\ncreated: 2026-08-23\\r\\ntags:\\r\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\r\\n  - \\"obsidian/plugins\\"\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nSource report behind [[Obsidian Plugin Atlas]].\\r\\n\\r\\n# Obsidian Community-Plugin Ecosystem: Deep Research Report for a 15,000-Note, 8 GB Mac Vault\\r\\n\\r\\n**Research snapshot:** 23 August 2026. The official Obsidian community directory is changing fast enough that different official-directory crawls taken only days apart report between **6,812 and 6,867 plugins**. I therefore use **about 6.8k current community plugins** as the defensible catalogue denominator rather than pretending the directory is a static dataset. The official `obsidian-releases` repository remains the canonical machine-readable catalogue, while the web directory adds categories, release history, downloads, compatibility, health/review signals, and links to repositories. cite turn0search0 turn22search12 turn0search1 turn0search4\\r\\n\\r\\n## Executive verdict and ideal stack\\r\\n\\r\\nThe central conclusion is that **the best Obsidian setup for your system is not a giant “best plugins” collection**. It is a relatively small always-on execution layer, surrounded by specialized tools that earn their memory/CPU/complexity costs. This conclusion is driven by three facts. First, your 15,000-note vault is already large enough that indexing can become material even without plugins; a community report involving roughly 14,000 notes documented long re-index operations in restricted mode. Second, plugins vary enormously in startup behaviour across machines: one Mac report measured Omnisearch at 59 ms, Dataview at 52 ms, Git at 44 ms, QuickAdd at 46 ms and Templater at 35 ms, while another 2026 cold-start report saw Note Toolbar around 904 ms, Templater 503 ms and Meta Bind 457 ms. These are user measurements, not universal benchmarks, but the variance makes local measurement essential. Third, Obsidian cannot strongly sandbox community plugins: a plugin inherits broad application-level privileges and may read files, make network requests or execute other capabilities available to Obsidian. cite turn15search4 turn15search16 turn15search13 turn8search0\\r\\n\\r\\nThe most important ecosystem change is that **Obsidian itself now replaces more plugin territory than it did a few years ago**. Bases provides local database-like views over Markdown properties; Canvas is native and stores open JSON Canvas files; Obsidian URI already exposes automation actions; the official Web Clipper captures content locally; and Obsidian 1.12 added a first-party command-line interface for scripting and external-tool integration. This substantially lowers the marginal value of old plugins whose main proposition was databases, URI automation, shell integration, simple capture, or visual boards. cite turn10search7 turn10search0 turn10search10 turn6search0 turn8search14 turn8search6 turn8search16\\r\\n\\r\\n**My recommended architecture is therefore:**\\r\\n\\r\\n| Tier | Recommended configuration | Why |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **Essential and always enabled** | **QuickAdd, Templater, Quick Switcher++, Linter, Tag Wrangler, Local REST API with MCP, Tasks** | These provide orchestration, deterministic content generation, fast navigation, metadata hygiene, agent interoperability and task execution without requiring heavyweight AI indexes. QuickAdd and Templater are complementary rather than duplicates: QuickAdd orchestrates capture/macros; Templater generates programmable note content. cite turn24search1 turn12search2 turn14search0 turn17view0 turn16search0 turn24search0 turn13search1 |\\r\\n| **High-value specialized** | **Notebook Navigator, Omnisearch, Excalidraw, Advanced Canvas, Modal Forms, Meta Bind, PDF++, Spaced Repetition, Git** | Install only where the corresponding workflow is real. Notebook Navigator and Omnisearch merit performance tests because both maintain additional representations of vault information. Excalidraw and Advanced Canvas are valuable only if visual work recurs. cite turn14search1 turn14search2 turn25view0 turn19search0 turn18search2 turn25view1 turn18search1 turn20search1 turn22search0 |\\r\\n| **Enabled only when needed** | **BRAT, an embedded-agent UI such as Claudian or Grimoire, Consistent Attachments and Links in audit-only mode** | BRAT is a beta-testing utility; leaving experimental code in your production stack has little benefit. Embedded agent UIs add convenience but duplicate Claude/Codex/Gemini capabilities you already possess. Consistent Attachments can audit portability without performing vault reorganisation. cite turn21search2 turn24search2 turn23search2 turn18search0 |\\r\\n| **Experimental** | **Smart Connections, TaskNotes, Datacore, Project Manager, Decks, Grimoire** | Each has genuine upside but either creates an index, adds many files/state, remains young, or overlaps stronger established/native options. cite turn23search1 turn13search0 turn12search3 turn13search6 turn20search3 turn23search2 |\\r\\n| **Not recommended for this configuration** | **Legacy Kanban, Text Extractor, archived Zotero Integration, generic AI-chat plugins, Shell Commands as a default automation layer, redundant cosmetic/navigation plugins** | Legacy Kanban and Zotero Integration have entered the Community Archive; Text Extractor explicitly says it is unmaintained; native CLI plus Local REST API/MCP substantially reduces Shell Commands\' unique value; generic AI chat adds little when Claude/Codex/Gemini already have direct access pathways. cite turn13search3 turn18search5 turn20search2 turn9search1 turn24search0 turn8search16 |\\r\\n\\r\\nFor **task management**, I would start with **Tasks rather than TaskNotes**. Tasks keeps tasks embedded in the Markdown notes where they naturally arise and maintains a vault-wide task index; TaskNotes is much richer—each task becomes a Markdown note with properties and its views use Bases—but that architecture can materially increase file count in an already 15,000-file system. TaskNotes becomes the better choice only when a task itself is a durable business object needing custom fields, dependencies, time tracking, calendars or direct AI-agent manipulation. cite turn13search1 turn13search0\\r\\n\\r\\nFor **AI integration**, I would install **no generic AI plugin by default**. The highest-value AI-related Obsidian plugin for your particular system is instead **Local REST API with MCP**. It exposes authenticated local REST and Streamable HTTP MCP endpoints for reading, writing, patching specific headings/frontmatter, searching, invoking commands and opening files. Claude Code can connect directly; other MCP clients can use the same interface. This gives external agents a clean Obsidian-native control plane without maintaining another embedding index or AI chat silo. cite turn24search0\\r\\n\\r\\nFor **structured data**, the default should now be **native Bases**, not Dataview. Bases reads local Markdown properties, supports filters, sorting, grouping, formulas and multiple views, and stores definitions in `.base` files. Dataview remains worthwhile when you specifically require DQL, DataviewJS, inline fields, sophisticated task queries or embedded computed views that Bases cannot yet express. Datacore is the interesting successor experiment, but its maintainer-described performance advantage remains a claim rather than a benchmark I would treat as established production evidence. cite turn10search7 turn10search0 turn10search3 turn12search1 turn12search3\\r\\n\\r\\n## Methodology, ecosystem census, and catalogue coverage\\r\\n\\r\\nI began with the official catalogue rather than a preselected “best plugin” list. The Obsidian community directory currently reports roughly 6.8k plugins; the canonical `community-plugins.json` is maintained in `obsidianmd/obsidian-releases`, while the directory layers on categories and release/download metadata. The downloadable statistics data is separate. Obsidian-community discussion indicates that displayed download figures derive from release-download statistics and are cumulative enough that old or abandoned plugins can retain impressive numbers, so I used adoption as a supporting signal rather than a quality score. cite turn0search0 turn0search1 turn0search8 turn0search3 turn0search9\\r\\n\\r\\nOne important limitation must be explicit: the catalogue JSON is approximately multi-megabyte scale and the GitHub connector would not return its full body in this research session. I could verify its existence, structure, current directory totals, taxonomy, sampled entries and a large set of individual listing pages, but I could **not honestly produce a 6,800-row, repository-by-repository code audit**. Consequently, this report is exhaustive at the **ecosystem census/taxonomy and competitive-landscape level**, explicitly grades **87 named plugins**, and deeply evaluates 30-plus serious or important negative candidates. The unnamed remainder is not being represented as individually verified; it is bulk-triaged under explicit exclusion rules below. This is materially more defensible than inventing per-plugin scores from metadata I could not retrieve.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe official directory\'s own taxonomy is highly overlapping. Recent snapshots show especially large populations in Integrations, Files, AI, Editing, Sidebar, Visualization, Automation and Links, while the fuller taxonomy also contains Backup, Data, Export, Git, Syncing, Code, CSS, PDF, Research, Flashcards, OCR, Canvas, Bases, Calendar, Project Management, Properties, Tables, Tasks, Collaboration, Publishing, Annotation, Autocomplete, Commands, Hotkeys, Review, Search, Templating, Vim and many more tags. A single plugin can belong to several tags, so these are not additive counts. cite turn0search0 turn4view0\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom those catalogue tags and the actual products encountered, the ecosystem resolves into the following practical competitive domains rather than a fixed “top ten categories” framework: **capture and ingestion; deterministic automation; programmable templating; navigation and switching; lexical retrieval; semantic retrieval; file/folder browsing; structured-data views; metadata editing; task execution; project management; calendars; visual thinking; native Canvas enhancement; PDF/research workflows; citations/reference managers; teaching and spaced repetition; writing/editing; formatting/hygiene; attachment management; version control; sync/backup; publishing; collaboration; external-service integration; command/interface customization; developer tooling; AI chat; AI coding-agent integration; local-agent bridges/MCP; OCR; media handling; and highly domain-specific niches such as music, finance, maps and tabletop gaming.** The breadth is supported by the catalogue\'s overlapping category structure rather than imposed before discovery. cite turn4view0\\r\\n\\r\\nThe research stages were:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Catalogue triage.** A plugin advanced when its basic purpose was plausibly relevant to a 15,000-note personal/business operating system, when it had a meaningful differentiator from Obsidian core or your existing AI agents, or when it was important enough that an explicit rejection was warranted. Low-adoption single-purpose wrappers, highly specific service integrations, purely cosmetic tools, obsolete core replacements and clear duplicates were demoted unless they revealed a new ecosystem category.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence escalation.** Serious candidates were checked against current official directory pages for versions, platforms, releases, adoption and repository links. Primary documentation was preferred over testimonials. For performance, maintainer claims are never treated as measurements; community measurements are labelled as anecdotes. For security, Obsidian\'s own plugin-security model governs the baseline threat assessment. cite turn8search0\\r\\n\\r\\n**Competitive elimination.** Native Obsidian capability was considered a competitor. That is why Bases now meaningfully reduces Dataview\'s score, native Canvas changes the value proposition of whiteboard plugins, the CLI/URI reduce the need for shell/URI wrappers, and the Web Clipper reduces the need for many capture extensions. cite turn10search7 turn10search10 turn8search16 turn6search0 turn8search14\\r\\n\\r\\n**Performance conservatism.** An unmeasured index is not called “fast.” A claimed 100,000-note capacity is not converted into a proven 15,000-note performance guarantee. Persistent filesystem watching, embeddings, OCR, Git polling, local servers and database indexes were penalized even when no benchmark demonstrated a problem. This is researcher inference based on architecture, not measured Mac performance.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence notation used below:** **V** = verified current listing/documentation; **M** = maintainer claim; **U** = user anecdote/measurement; **I** = researcher inference; **?** = behaviour not adequately verified.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Catalogue-wide preliminary grading and exclusions\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following 87-plugin table is the broad, named census produced by the catalogue pass. Names and catalogue IDs were taken from or cross-checked against the official directory/catalogue wherever the retrieval system exposed them; entries marked **ID?** are intentionally left unverified rather than guessed. The numerical score is a preliminary research judgement, not a crowdsourced rating. Official catalogue existence and taxonomy are grounded in the Obsidian directory and catalogue repository. cite turn0search1 turn4view0\\r\\n\\r\\n| Plugin | Community ID | Discovered domain | Preliminary | Maintenance signal | Adoption signal | Decision |\\r\\n|---|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|\\r\\n| QuickAdd | `quickadd` | Capture/automation | **95** | Active | Very high | **Advance** — foundational orchestration |\\r\\n| Templater | `templater-obsidian` | Programmable templating | **93** | Active | Very high | **Advance** |\\r\\n| Linter | `obsidian-linter` | Hygiene/formatting | **89** | Active | Very high | **Advance** |\\r\\n| Tag Wrangler | `tag-wrangler` | Metadata/tags | **88** | Active | Very high | **Advance** |\\r\\n| Local REST API with MCP | `obsidian-local-rest-api` | Automation/agents | **94** | Active | High | **Advance** |\\r\\n| Quick Switcher++ | `darlal-switcher-plus` | Navigation | **92** | Active | High | **Advance** |\\r\\n| Notebook Navigator | `notebook-navigator` | Browsing/navigation | **90** | Very active | High | **Advance; benchmark** |\\r\\n| Omnisearch | `omnisearch` | Full-text retrieval | **86** | Active | Very high | **Advance; benchmark index** |\\r\\n| Tasks | `obsidian-tasks-plugin` | Tasks | **88** | Active | Very high | **Advance; category winner** |\\r\\n| TaskNotes | `tasknotes` | Tasks/operations | **87** | Very active | High | **Experiment; richer Tasks alternative** |\\r\\n| Project Manager | `project-manager` | Project management | **79** | Young/active | Moderate | **Experiment** |\\r\\n| Dataview | `dataview` | Data/query | **76** | Stable/slower | Very high | **Retain where necessary; Bases first** |\\r\\n| Datacore | `datacore` | Data/query | **73** | WIP | Moderate | **Experiment only** |\\r\\n| Meta Bind | `obsidian-meta-bind-plugin` | Interactive metadata | **84** | Active | High | **Advance** |\\r\\n| Modal Forms | `modalforms` | Structured capture | **85** | Very active | Moderate | **Advance** |\\r\\n| Lazy Loader | `lazy-plugins` | Performance | **83** | Active | High | **Advance only after baseline** |\\r\\n| Note Toolbar | `note-toolbar` | Commands/interface | **77** | Very active | High | **Experiment** |\\r\\n| Various Complements | `various-complements` | Autocomplete | **70** | Active | High | **Optional** |\\r\\n| Excalidraw | `obsidian-excalidraw-plugin` | Visual thinking | **88** | Very active | Extremely high | **Advance** |\\r\\n| Advanced Canvas | `advanced-canvas` | Canvas/visualization | **85** | Very active | High | **Advance if Canvas is recurrent** |\\r\\n| tldraw | `tldraw` | Drawing | **75** | Active | Moderate | **Runner-up to Excalidraw** |\\r\\n| ExcaliBrain | `excalibrain` | Knowledge graph | **70** | Active | Moderate | **Optional; dependency-heavy** |\\r\\n| Spaced Repetition | `obsidian-spaced-repetition` | Learning/review | **82** | Active | High | **Advance for teaching/learning** |\\r\\n| Decks | `decks` | Learning/review | **79** | Young/very active | Low–moderate | **Experiment** |\\r\\n| PDF++ | `pdf-plus` | PDF/research | **74** | Major rewrite underway | High | **Specialized** |\\r\\n| Consistent Attachments and Links | `consistent-attachments-and-links` | Portability/hygiene | **78** | Very active | Moderate | **Audit mode first** |\\r\\n| Git | `obsidian-git` | Version control | **78** | Very active | Very high | **Desktop specialized** |\\r\\n| Folder notes | `folder-notes` | Folder navigation | **76** | Active | High | **Optional** |\\r\\n| BRAT | `obsidian42-brat` | Beta testing | **68** | Active | Very high | **On demand only** |\\r\\n| Shell commands | `obsidian-shellcommands` | External automation | **61** | Old release | Moderate | **Mostly superseded** |\\r\\n| Smart Connections | `smart-connections` | Semantic retrieval | **80** | Very active | Very high | **High-upside experiment** |\\r\\n| Grimoire | `grimoire` | Local CLI agents | **77** | New/very active | Low | **Experiment** |\\r\\n| Claudian | `realclaudian` | Local CLI agents | **78** | Current listing | Extremely high | **Best mature AI-UI experiment** |\\r\\n| Copilot | `copilot` | AI agents | **74** | Current V4 | Extremely high | **Do not default-install** |\\r\\n| Claude Code IDE | `claude-code-ide` | Read-only agent context | **81** | Young/active | Low–moderate | **Strong specialized option** |\\r\\n| Text Extractor | `text-extractor` | OCR/extraction | **42** | **Explicitly unmaintained** | High | **Reject** |\\r\\n| Zotero Integration | `obsidian-zotero-desktop-connector` | Research/citations | **45** | **Community Archive** | High | **Reject for new install** |\\r\\n| Kanban | `obsidian-kanban` | Kanban/project | **40** | **Community Archive** | Very high | **Reject** |\\r\\n| Advanced Tables | `table-editor-obsidian` | Tables/editing | **58** | Not deeply rechecked | Very high | **Native features reduce need** |\\r\\n| Calendar | `calendar` | Dates/calendar | **58** | Not deeply rechecked | Very high | **Usually redundant in new stack** |\\r\\n| Periodic Notes | `periodic-notes` | Periodic planning | **58** | Not deeply rechecked | High | **Only for explicit periodic-note workflow** |\\r\\n| Style Settings | `obsidian-style-settings` | Appearance | **62** | Current/popular | Very high | **Theme-dependent** |\\r\\n| Iconize | `obsidian-icon-folder` | Appearance/navigation | **58** | Current/popular | Very high | **Cosmetic; low priority** |\\r\\n| Remotely Save | `remotely-save` | Sync | **64** | Popular/current | Very high | **Only if chosen as sole sync layer** |\\r\\n| Editing Toolbar | `obsidian-editing-toolbar` | Editing UI | **52** | Popular | Very high | **Low uniqueness** |\\r\\n| Minimal Theme Settings | `obsidian-minimal-settings` | Theme settings | **54** | Popular | Very high | **Only with Minimal theme** |\\r\\n| Importer | `obsidian-importer` | Import/migration | **63** | Officially prominent | High | **Install when migrating, then disable** |\\r\\n| Outliner | `obsidian-outliner` | Outlining | **67** | Established | High | **Optional** |\\r\\n| Homepage | `homepage` | Launch/navigation | **61** | Established | Very high | **Notebook Navigator/workspaces can replace** |\\r\\n| Recent Files | `recent-files-obsidian` | Navigation | **61** | Established | Very high | **Usually duplicated by Navigator** |\\r\\n| Admonition | `obsidian-admonition` | Formatting | **35** | Legacy relevance | High | **Core callouts make it unnecessary for most** |\\r\\n| Advanced URI | `obsidian-advanced-uri` | Automation | **62** | Established | High | **CLI + native URI + REST API now stronger** |\\r\\n| Commander | `cmdr` | Interface/commands | **72** | Established | High | **Optional; Note Toolbar alternative** |\\r\\n| Natural Language Dates | `nldates-obsidian` | Dates/input | **68** | Established | High | **Useful if date-entry friction is real** |\\r\\n| Breadcrumbs | `breadcrumbs` | Hierarchies | **62** | Not deeply rechecked | Moderate | **Optional/complex** |\\r\\n| Hider | `obsidian-hider` | Appearance | **55** | Popular | High writing | **65** | Established | Moderate | **Niche to long manuscripts** |\\r\\n| Digital Garden | `obsidian-digital-garden` | Publishing | **63** | Established | Moderate | **Only if publishing model fits** |\\r\\n| Custom Frames | `obsidian-custom-frames` | Integrations/UI | **58** | Established | Moderate | **Web-app embedding rarely essential** |\\r\\n| Hover Editor | `obsidian-hover-editor` | Navigation/editing | **68** | Established | High | **Optional** |\\r\\n| Waypoint | `waypoint` | MOCs/folder graph | **65** | Established | Moderate | **Folder-architecture niche** |\\r\\n| GridExplorer | `gridexplorer` | Visual file browsing | **68** | Active | Moderate | **Navigator competitor; do not stack by default** |\\r\\n| OCR Extractor | `ocr-extractor` | OCR | candidate than archived integration** |\\r\\n| ZotFlow | `zotflow` | Zotero/PDF | **71** | Active-looking | Low–moderate | **Promising experiment** |\\r\\n| Mnemoloop | `mnemoloop` | Spaced repetition ## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"05a189","wall_time_seconds":0.2568029,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":7925,"output":"| Colored Tags Wrangler | `colored-tags-wrangler` | Appearance/tags | **58** | Current | Moderate | **Cosmetic companion only** |\\r\\n| Simple Folder Note | `simple-folder-note` | Folder navigation | **52** | Very new | Very low | **Folder notes stronger** |\\r\\n\\r\\nCurrent primary pages confirm the important positive and negative signals underlying this table. In particular, QuickAdd is at v2.22.0 with 205 releases and about 2M downloads; Tag Wrangler v0.6.5 is still being updated; Linter v1.32.0 remains active; Excalidraw v2.26.4 has hundreds of releases; Advanced Canvas v6.5.2 was updated within roughly a day of its catalogue snapshot; Meta Bind v1.5.1 is current; and Git v2.39.0 was updated within days. Conversely, legacy Kanban has moved to the Community Archive, Text Extractor explicitly declares itself unmaintained, archived Zotero Integration has not been updated in roughly two years, and the older Folder Note alternatives have been untouched for years. cite turn24search1 turn16search0 turn17view0 turn25view0 turn19search0 turn25view1 turn22search0 turn13search3 turn20search2 turn18search5 turn21search1 turn21search10\\r\\n\\r\\n**How the unnamed catalogue remainder was treated.** Plugins that did not advance fall predominantly into one of six explicit buckets: a narrow external-service connector irrelevant to your operating system; theme/cosmetic customization with negligible workflow impact; a one-function wrapper now covered by Obsidian core; a young plugin with almost no independent evidence; an older duplicate whose category now has a stronger maintained winner; or a domain-specific plugin—music, tabletop gaming, language-specific utilities, specialist finance/maps/etc.—with no plausible repeated value to your stated workflows. This is a bulk triage, **not an assertion that every unnamed plugin is low quality**.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Serious-candidate scorecard and detailed reviews\\r\\n\\r\\nScores implement your rubric directly: workflow impact 30 points, reliability/maintenance 15, performance efficiency 15, uniqueness against Obsidian/AI agents 15, large-vault suitability 10, privacy/security 10, and interface/usability 5. An unmeasured performance claim loses points rather than receiving assumed credit.\\r\\n\\r\\nClassification bands are: **92–100 TRANSFORMATIVE; 86–91 HIGH-IMPACT INSTALL; 78–85 STRONG CANDIDATE; 70–77 PROMISING EXPERIMENT; 60–69 NICHE OR OPTIONAL; 50–59 LOW-VALUE; below 50 NOT RECOMMENDED**, with **ABANDONED OR UNSAFE** overriding score where maintenance/security evidence warrants it.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe official-listing citation in each row is also the safest gateway to its **current official repository**, because several Obsidian projects have changed owners or repositories over time. “Development” below uses the latest current release/update signal when a reliable last meaningful commit could not be uniformly established.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Plugin / ID | Current evidence | Core value and best use | Activity / performance profile | Privacy / large vault / mobile | Main weakness & strongest competitor | Score / classification / confidence |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **QuickAdd** `quickadd` | v2.22.0; updated within week; 205 releases; ~2M; desktop/mobile. Official listing → repository. cite turn24search1 | Capture, templates, macros and multi-step automation. **Daily**. Best for session capture, student-note creation, research inboxes and business workflows. | Mostly startup registration + **manual-triggered** work. Low expected idle cost. | Local unless your macro calls external code/services. Excellent 15k fit by architecture. Mobile supported. | Complexity can migrate into opaque macros. Native Templates/CLI handle simpler cases. | **95 TRANSFORMATIVE — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Local REST API with MCP** `obsidian-local-rest-api` | v5.1.0; updated ~3 weeks; 72 releases; ~677k; desktop. cite turn24search0 | Gives Claude/Codex/scripts authenticated CRUD, targeted Markdown/frontmatter patching, search, commands and MCP. **Daily for agent-heavy use.** | Starts localhost server; small persistent background surface; substantive work is **request/AI-session triggered**. | HTTPS+self-signed cert/API key on localhost. Entire vault is potentially accessible to authenticated clients. Desktop only. | Powerful privilege boundary; requires careful token/port handling. Native CLI is safer for simpler automation. | **94 TRANSFORMATIVE — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Templater** `templater-obsidian` | v2.25.0; updated ~2 weeks; ~5.3M; desktop/mobile. cite turn12search2 | Programmable templates, JavaScript and dynamic note creation. **Daily** in standardized curriculum/business records. | Startup registration; note/template/edit triggers. Generally low idle load, but user scripts can do anything. | Local by default; arbitrary JavaScript/system-command capability is a security boundary. | Higher technical complexity than QuickAdd/native Templates. | **93 TRANSFORMATIVE — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Quick Switcher++** `darlal-switcher-plus` | v6.1.6; updated ~3 weeks; 88 releases; ~437k; desktop/mobile. cite turn14search0 | Keyboard navigation across files, headings, symbols, tags, links, panes, commands and related content. **Constant daily use.** | Light startup registration; **manual search triggered**. No heavyweight semantic index. | Local. Architecture is unusually attractive for 15k notes/8GB. Mobile supported. | Does not replace full-text content search. Omnisearch is the content-search complement. | **92 TRANSFORMATIVE — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Notebook Navigator** `notebook-navigator` | v3.3.4 in captured listing; updated within days; ~873k; desktop/mobile. cite turn14search1 | Powerful two-pane note browser with folders/tags/properties/recent/calendar/bulk operations. **Daily** if adopted as primary navigator. | Builds/uses a local cache and listens to vault changes; moderate expected overhead. | Maintainer says note content/file metadata stay local apart from documented update/remote-image behaviour; maintainer claims 100k+ note capability. One mobile user reported material improvement after removing it, so large-vault/mobile evidence is conflicting. cite turn14search1 turn15search9 | More UI/caching than core Explorer. Quick Switcher++ is leaner for keyboard users. | **90 HIGH-IMPACT — medium-high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Linter** `obsidian-linter` | v1.32.0; updated ~2 months; 85 releases; ~1M; desktop/mobile. cite turn17view0 | Enforces Markdown/YAML consistency. Particularly valuable when humans and agents both edit 15k files. **Daily but invisible.** | **Save/edit/manual triggered.** Avoid vault-wide automatic passes until rules are validated. | Local. Excellent large-vault architecture when scoped to edited files. | Conflicting rules can rewrite content unexpectedly; maintainer docs explicitly warn overlapping rules can behave undesirably. cite turn17view0 | **89 HIGH-IMPACT — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Tag Wrangler** `tag-wrangler` | v0.6.5; updated within days; 39 releases; ~1M; desktop/mobile. cite turn16search0 | Rename, reorganize, search and manage tags plus tag pages. **Weekly/daily** for ontology hygiene. | Mostly **manual-triggered**; low persistent cost expected. | Local. Very attractive large-vault value because tag cleanup otherwise becomes painful. | Renaming modifies many notes and is potentially irreversible; maintainer explicitly recommends backup before mass rename. cite turn16search0 | **88 HIGH-IMPACT — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Tasks** `obsidian-tasks-plugin` | v8.3.0; updated ~4 weeks; 141 releases; ~4M; desktop/mobile. cite turn13search1 | Vault-wide queries over normal Markdown checkboxes, recurrence and scheduling. **Daily**. | Maintains task information across vault; query/render work is view triggered. Docs caution that rendering hundreds/thousands of tasks can slow editing. cite turn13search1 | Local. Does not multiply your file count. Good 15k fit if queries are constrained. | Rich project/task metadata is weaker than TaskNotes. | **88 HIGH-IMPACT — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Excalidraw** `obsidian-excalidraw-plugin` | v2.26.4; updated within days; 576 releases; ~7.1M; desktop/mobile. cite turn25view0 | Freeform visual thinking, diagrams, transclusions, links and automation. **Several times/week if visual work is real.** | Heavy codebase but much work is **view/drawing triggered**; maintainer dynamically loads components partly to improve startup. Large/complex drawings and cached images can consume resources. cite turn17view1 | Works locally/offline by default. Optional AI/OCR, external images and script library can make network requests; user scripts execute code. cite turn17view1 | Enormous feature surface and learning curve. Native Canvas is simpler. | **88 HIGH-IMPACT — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **TaskNotes** `tasknotes` | v4.12.3 in captured listing; updated within week; ~1M. cite turn13search0 | Makes every task a Markdown note with properties; Bases-powered list/Kanban/calendar/agenda plus dependencies, calendar integration and time tracking. | Vault/index activity follows files and Bases; creates many additional notes. | Local task data by default; calendar sync is optional external integration. File proliferation is the main 15k concern. | Tasks is leaner; TaskNotes wins when tasks must be durable data objects. | **87 HIGH-IMPACT — medium-high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Omnisearch** `omnisearch` | v1.30.1; updated ~3 weeks; 162 releases; ~1.7M; desktop/mobile. cite turn14search2 | BM25/fuzzy full-text retrieval over vault, with optional PDF/Office/image extraction. **Daily** if core search is inadequate. | Maintains local full-text index; initial indexing and update work are real. A Mac anecdote measured ~59 ms startup, while Android reports show initial indexing can be problematic. cite turn15search16 turn15search11 | Local indexing; optional local HTTP server. Good desktop candidate but must be measured on 15k/8GB. | Quick Switcher++ is much lighter when you know the note/header. | **86 HIGH-IMPACT — medium-high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Advanced Canvas** `advanced-canvas` | v6.5.2; updated ~23 hours in listing snapshot; 102 releases; ~744k; desktop/mobile. cite turn19search0 | Turns native Canvas into serious flowchart/presentation/metadata workspace while preserving JSON Canvas compatibility. | Most work **view/canvas triggered**; graph metadata integration and complex canvases increase work. | Local. Large-vault effect is usually bounded by canvases opened rather than every Markdown file, though metadata integration broadens scope. | Excalidraw is better freeform visual thinking; use Advanced Canvas only for Canvas-native structured diagrams. | **85 STRONG — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Modal Forms** `modalforms` | v1.70.1 appeared in the recent-update feed; active development; desktop/mobile listing. cite turn11view2 turn18search2 | Typed forms that return structured data to templates/QuickAdd/JavaScript or create notes directly. **Frequent** for student-session distillates, product decisions and standardized records. | **Manual-triggered** forms; low expected idle cost. | Local unless downstream scripts transmit data. Good large-vault fit because work is targeted. | Extra schema layer; simpler prompts may be handled by QuickAdd alone. | **85 STRONG — medium-high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Meta Bind** `obsidian-meta-bind-plugin` | v1.5.1; updated ~2 months; 141 releases; ~467–484k; desktop/mobile. cite turn25view1 turn16search1 | Interactive fields/buttons bound directly to frontmatter. Ideal for operational dashboards and editable records. | **Note-open/edit triggered**; can become expensive if a note contains many reactive controls. One user cold-start report measured ~457 ms, illustrating the need for local measurement. cite turn15search13 | Local. Good large-vault fit when controls are concentrated in selected notes. | Bases already edits properties in tables; Meta Bind earns its place only for in-note interaction. | **84 STRONG — medium-high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Lazy Loader** `lazy-plugins` | v1.0.24; updated ~3 months; ~131k; desktop/mobile. cite turn19search2 | Defers nonessential plugin loading. | Startup orchestration only; deliberately shifts work later. A 5,000-note/~30-plugin user benchmark reported mean “startup to Dataview fully rendered” improving from 2.22 s to 1.41 s across 20 openings. That is encouraging but not universal. cite turn15search15 | Local. Large-vault neutral; most useful when plugin startup, not core indexing, is the bottleneck. | Can create dependency/load-order surprises and disguise rather than remove work. | **83 STRONG — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **Spaced Repetition** `obsidian-spaced-repetition` | v1.15.4; updated ~2 months; 81 releases; ~581k; desktop/mobile. cite turn20search1 | FSRS/SM-2 flashcards plus whole-note review. Strong fit for curriculum review, memorization and teaching-material QA. | Review/manual work; some indexing of eligible cards/notes. | Local; established mobile support. | Adds flashcard syntax into notes. Decks offers a newer structural-Markdown approach. | **82 STRONG — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Claude Code IDE** `claude-code-ide` | v0.2.5; updated ~3 months; 7 releases; ~14k; desktop. cite turn24search3 | Gives Claude Code current file/open-file/selection context over a local WebSocket bridge. **Frequent when coding/agent work occurs.** | Persistent local bridge while enabled; actual work is **AI-session triggered**. | Read-only context surface, local connection, per-session token; provider handling occurs through Claude Code. | Narrower than Local REST API: excellent safer context bridge, but no general vault mutation API. | **81 STRONG — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Smart Connections** `smart-connections` | v4.7.2; updated ~2 weeks; 375 releases; ~1.1M; desktop/mobile. cite turn23search1 | Local embeddings for semantically related notes and natural-language lookup. **Potentially daily** if it finds forgotten material lexical search misses. | **Initial embedding/index work + persistent vault-change watching.** This is precisely the architecture that deserves an 8GB-Mac benchmark. | Embeddings are local by default; source exclusions exist. Maintainer describes `.smart-env` index/environment. cite turn23search1 | External Claude/Gemini can already perform semantic analysis once context is supplied; value depends on retrieval quality, not chat. | **80 STRONG — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **Decks** `decks` | v2.9.2; updated within week; ~150 releases but only ~9k adoption; desktop/mobile. cite turn20search3 | FSRS flashcards using structural Markdown, Canvas cards, exams, analytics and migration from Spaced Repetition. | Maintains SQLite state plus small sync logs; review/manual triggered with background persistence. | Local core; optional AI. Good architecture but less field history than Spaced Repetition. | Younger and more stateful than Spaced Repetition. | **79 STRONG — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **Project Manager** `project-manager` | v1.8.0; updated ~2 months; ~55k; created only months ago. cite turn13search6 | Table/Gantt/Kanban/dependencies/custom project fields over Markdown/YAML. | View/data triggered; young architecture. | Maintainer describes local/offline operation. Large-vault evidence still limited. | TaskNotes/Bases can cover much of the same territory with broader momentum. | **79 STRONG — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **Git** `obsidian-git` | v2.39.0; updated within days; 150 releases; ~3M; desktop/mobile. cite turn22search0 | Version history, diffs, commit/pull/push and scheduled backup. | Git scans and optional scheduled network/process work; avoid hyper-frequent auto-sync on a large vault. | Local repository can remain private; pushing transmits tracked content to your configured remote. Mobile implementation is explicitly described by the maintainer as unstable and memory-constrained. cite turn22search0 | Native Git CLI/desktop clients can do the same job without plugin startup cost. | **78 STRONG — high confidence desktop; low mobile** |\\r\\n| **Consistent Attachments and Links** `consistent-attachments-and-links` | v3.35.3; updated within a week; 166 releases; ~138k; desktop/mobile. cite turn18search0 | Audits bad links and can make Markdown paths/attachments portable to outside editors/GitHub. | File-event/manual bulk operations; potentially extensive writes when conversion is run. | Local. Audit mode is excellent; migration operations carry data-integrity risk. | Obsidian\'s own flexible link resolution is easier inside Obsidian. For your rule set, **do not perform moves/deletions during testing**. | **78 STRONG, audit use — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Note Toolbar** `note-toolbar` | v1.34.13; updated ~2 weeks; 543 releases; ~314k; desktop/mobile. cite turn19search1 | Context-sensitive note toolbars for commands, scripts, files and menus. | Note-open/UI work; scripting optional. One cold-start anecdote measured ~904 ms, so test locally. cite turn15search13 | Local except URLs/scripts you configure. | Command palette + QuickAdd already provide many actions; can create interface clutter. | **77 PROMISING — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **Grimoire** `grimoire` | v1.1.7; updated ~3 days; 43 releases in ~3 months; ~3k; desktop. cite turn23search2 | Runs Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI and other local CLI agents in one Obsidian side panel. | **AI-session/external-process triggered**; potentially high transient CPU/memory depending agent/CLI. | No Grimoire backend/telemetry according to maintainer; chosen providers still receive whatever their CLI sends. | Very new. Claudian has much more adoption; external terminals already provide same agents. | **77 PROMISING — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **Folder notes** `folder-notes` | v1.8.26; updated ~3 months; 123 releases; ~412k; desktop/mobile. cite turn21search0 | Makes folders navigable as notes/MOCs. | File-explorer/event-driven, modest expected cost. | Local. Works naturally with hierarchical business/curriculum vaults. | Notebook Navigator reduces the need for folder-page machinery. | **76 PROMISING — medium-high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Dataview** `dataview` | v0.5.68; last directory update roughly a year; ~4.8M; desktop/mobile. cite turn12search1 | Powerful vault metadata index and DQL/JS query engine. | **Startup index + persistent metadata updates + view rendering.** Broad queries can be costly. | Local. DataviewJS executes powerful code; normal DQL is more constrained. | Native Bases now covers a large proportion of ordinary database-view needs. | **76 PROMISING/LEGACY POWER TOOL — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **tldraw** `tldraw` | v1.31.0; updated roughly yesterday; ~89k; desktop/mobile. cite turn16search10 | Modern vector drawing stored as Markdown-compatible drawing files. | Primarily **view-triggered**. | Local-oriented; good mobile story. | Excalidraw has much deeper Obsidian-specific integrations and adoption. | **75 PROMISING — medium-high confidence** |\\r\\n| **PDF++** `pdf-plus` | v0.40.31; listing says ~12 months since release; 254 releases; ~719k; desktop/mobile. Maintainer says a major 1.0 refactor is underway. cite turn18search1 | Native PDF viewer enhancement and Markdown-backed annotations. | PDF-view triggered; writing annotations directly into PDFs is optional and experimental. | Backlink annotation mode keeps notes in Markdown. | Development gap/refactor lowers reliability confidence; avoid direct PDF modification initially. | **74 PROMISING — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **Datacore** `datacore` | v0.1.29; updated ~5 months; ~316k. cite turn12search3 | Dataview successor with editable React data views. | Maintainer claims 2–10× faster query/render performance, but that is not independent measurement; still indexes vault data. cite turn12search3 | Local; large-vault promise is attractive but insufficiently independently proven. | WIP status + native Bases. | **73 PROMISING — medium-low confidence** |\\r\\n| **ExcaliBrain** `excalibrain` | v0.2.18; updated ~3 months; ~332k; desktop/mobile. cite turn16search9 | Structured relationship mind map using links/tags/YAML. | Requires both Dataview and Excalidraw, compounding index/code load. | Local. | Dependency/complexity cost is high relative to native graph/Canvas. | **70 PROMISING/NICHE — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **Various Complements** `various-complements` | v11.4.0; updated ~2 months; ~563k; desktop/mobile. cite turn14search3 | IDE-like autocomplete. | **Edit-triggered**. One user cold-start report measured ~260 ms. cite turn15search13 | Local. | AI/text expansion and native suggestions reduce uniqueness. | **70 PROMISING/NICHE — medium confidence** |\\r\\n| **BRAT** `obsidian42-brat` | v2.2.0; updated ~2 months; ~976k; desktop/mobile. cite turn21search2 | Beta plugin/theme installer. | Startup/update/network work. | Downloads arbitrary beta code from configured GitHub repositories; therefore increases software-supply-chain exposure by design. | No daily workflow benefit when not beta testing. | **68 NICHE — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Shell commands** `obsidian-shellcommands` | v0.23.0; last directory update roughly 2 years; desktop only. cite turn9search1 | Launches external commands from Obsidian. | **External-process/manual/event triggered.** | Arbitrary system-command execution is inherently high privilege. | Native Obsidian CLI + external agents + Local REST API now cover most high-value use cases more cleanly. | **61 NICHE — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Zotero Integration** `obsidian-zotero-desktop-connector` | v3.2.1; last update ~2 years; now Community Archive; ~537k. cite turn18search5 | Historically excellent Zotero citations/import. | Desktop/Zotero integration. | Local Zotero workflow. | Archived. Zotero Sync/ZotFlow are more plausible new-install directions. | **45 ABANDONED / NOT RECOMMENDED — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Text Extractor** `text-extractor` | v0.7.0; page explicitly states **Unmaintained**; ~319k. cite turn20search2 | OCR/PDF text extraction companion. | OCR is resource-intensive; processing is on demand and cached. | OCR local, language packs downloaded; mobile extraction does not work. | Explicit abandonment + known PDF failures. | **42 ABANDONED — high confidence** |\\r\\n| **Kanban** `obsidian-kanban` | v2.0.51; last update ~2 years; ~2.5M; Community Archive/seeking maintainers. cite turn13search3 | Markdown Kanban boards. | View/edit triggered. | Local. | Maintenance status is unacceptable for a foundational operational board when TaskNotes/Bases/newer alternatives exist. | **40 ABANDONED / NOT RECOMMENDED — high confidence** |\\r\\n\\r\\nSeveral competitive conclusions emerge from that scorecard.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Capture and workflow automation:** QuickAdd is the clear winner. Modal Forms is not a replacement; it is a complementary structured-input layer. Templater is likewise complementary when generated content needs JavaScript or complex deterministic logic. This three-plugin combination should exist only where each has a distinct role: **Modal Forms asks, Templater renders, QuickAdd orchestrates.** cite turn24search1 turn18search2 turn12search2\\r\\n\\r\\n**Navigation:** Quick Switcher++ is the lean keyboard winner. Notebook Navigator is the visual-browsing winner. They can coexist because one replaces repeated keyboard searching while the other can replace File Explorer, recent-files plugins, calendar browsing and some bulk metadata tools. GridExplorer, Recent Files and Homepage become difficult to justify once Notebook Navigator is accepted. cite turn14search0 turn14search1 turn21search8\\r\\n\\r\\n**Retrieval:** Omnisearch wins predictable lexical/full-text retrieval; Smart Connections wins semantic retrieval. They are not identical, but I would not automatically run both on an 8 GB machine. Start with Omnisearch. Add Smart Connections only after demonstrating queries where semantic matching reliably surfaces notes that Omnisearch and agent-driven grep/search do not. cite turn14search2 turn23search1\\r\\n\\r\\n**Visual thinking:** Excalidraw is the overall winner because its Obsidian integration is extraordinarily deep—links, transclusion, automation, scripts, local drawing storage and mobile support. Advanced Canvas is the winner specifically for **structured native-Canvas work** such as process maps, presentations and metadata-aware diagrams. tldraw is the strongest cleaner alternative when Excalidraw\'s enormous feature surface is undesirable. cite turn17view1 turn19search0 turn16search10\\r\\n\\r\\n**Learning:** Spaced Repetition wins today on longevity and adoption. Decks is the strongest architectural challenger because it avoids special inline flashcard syntax and adds richer review/exam infrastructure, but it introduces SQLite state and has much less field history. cite turn20search1 turn20search3\\r\\n\\r\\n**Git:** Obsidian Git remains the feature winner on desktop, but the plugin itself warns that mobile Git is unstable and can crash or run indefinitely because of memory limitations in the JavaScript Git implementation. Git Viewer is an interesting newer minimalist alternative because it deliberately omits pull/push/reset and focuses on local status/history/selected-file commits, but its adoption is tiny. cite turn22search0 turn21search3\\r\\n\\r\\n## Competitive AI, performance, privacy, and large-vault analysis\\r\\n\\r\\nThe **AI-plugin ecosystem is now one of the largest Obsidian categories**, and this is precisely where popularity is least informative for you. Smart Connections has passed roughly 1.1M downloads; Copilot roughly 1.7M; Claudian roughly 1.8M in its current agent-oriented listing; meanwhile new local-CLI integrations such as Grimoire appear rapidly. Yet you already use Claude, Codex and Gemini, so “there is a chat box inside Obsidian” deserves almost no uniqueness credit. cite turn23search1 turn23search5 turn24search2 turn23search2\\r\\n\\r\\nThe AI plugins fall into three fundamentally different architectures:\\r\\n\\r\\n| AI architecture | Strongest candidates | What genuinely adds value | Main concern | Verdict |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **Semantic vault index** | Smart Connections | Retrieval by meaning without manually specifying context; local embeddings by default. cite turn23search1 | Initial embedding work, index memory, continuous change watching; retrieval quality must beat lexical search. | **Test only after Omnisearch/core search leaves a demonstrated gap.** |\\r\\n| **Embedded coding-agent UI** | Claudian, Grimoire, Copilot | Inline edits/diffs, note/folder mentions, agent sessions inside the writing surface. Claudian explicitly embeds Claude Code/Codex and other agents; Grimoire supports a wide set of local CLI agents and says it has no hosted backend. cite turn24search2 turn23search2 turn23search5 | External agent processes, provider data transmission, substantial overlap with terminal-native workflows. | **Choose at most one; enabled on demand. Claudian first, Grimoire if provider breadth matters.** |\\r\\n| **Obsidian control plane for external agents** | Local REST API with MCP, Claude Code IDE | Lets existing agents interact with Obsidian rather than replacing the agent. REST API offers full authenticated mutation/search/commands; Claude Code IDE exposes narrower read-only live editor context. cite turn24search0 turn24search3 | REST API is highly privileged; Claude Code IDE is much narrower. | **Best fit for your existing agent ecosystem.** |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Claudian** is more interesting than a normal AI plugin because its current product is not merely “Claude chat”: it embeds Claude Code, Codex and other local-agent harnesses with file read/write/search, shell workflows and inline diffs. That Obsidian-native interface creates real marginal value even when you already subscribe to/use the underlying agents. The weakness is that the underlying providers still receive inputs, attached files/images and tool outputs according to the selected provider\'s behaviour. cite turn24search2\\r\\n\\r\\n**Grimoire** is conceptually even better aligned with a multi-agent user because it supports Claude Code, Codex, legacy Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Kimi, Grok, Qwen and other local CLIs in one UI and says there is no Grimoire proxy/backend/telemetry. But it was created only months ago and had roughly 3,000 downloads in the researched snapshot, so confidence in long-term reliability is much lower than Claudian\'s. cite turn23search2\\r\\n\\r\\n**Copilot** has the greatest adoption among the conventional AI-agent contenders and its V4 product has moved toward Claude Code/Codex/OpenCode-style agents. That is a meaningful improvement over generic RAG chat. Nevertheless, in your environment it competes directly with agents you already use and therefore needs a specific winning workflow before it deserves to remain installed. cite turn23search5\\r\\n\\r\\n**Smart Connections is the one AI plugin I would judge on retrieval rather than model quality.** Its core now creates embeddings locally by default, automatically maintains an index and exposes related-notes/semantic lookup. That is genuinely different from “ask Claude a question” because the difficult problem is discovering which 15,000-note subset should enter context in the first place. Its drawback is exactly the same architecture: local embeddings and continuous index maintenance cost compute and storage. The plugin supports exclusions, which is important for student records or other folders you do not want indexed. cite turn23search1\\r\\n\\r\\nFor an **8 GB Mac**, architecture matters more than download count. I would classify serious candidates this way:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Performance class | Candidates | Why |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **Low expected persistent cost** | QuickAdd, Quick Switcher++, Tag Wrangler, Modal Forms | Work occurs mainly when you invoke a command/form rather than continuously. cite turn24search1 turn14search0 turn16search0 turn18search2 |\\r\\n| **Low to moderate** | Templater, Linter, Local REST API, Note Toolbar, Meta Bind, Folder notes | Startup registration plus event/note activity; REST API keeps a localhost server; Meta Bind/Toolbar can make individual notes more reactive. cite turn12search2 turn17view0 turn24search0 turn19search1 turn25view1 turn21search0 |\\r\\n| **Moderate/indexed** | Tasks, Dataview, Notebook Navigator, Omnisearch | Maintain vault-level task/metadata/cache/search representations. cite turn13search1 turn12search1 turn14search1 turn14search2 |\\r\\n| **High initial or potentially persistent** | Smart Connections; Omnisearch with broad attachment/OCR indexing; aggressive Git auto-sync | Embeddings/full-text indexing or scheduled filesystem/network work. cite turn23search1 turn14search2 turn22search0 |\\r\\n| **High transient, view/session triggered** | Excalidraw on complex drawings, huge Advanced Canvas files, local CLI AI agents | Heavy code/rendering or external agent processes exist, but only while the relevant view/session is active. cite turn17view1 turn19search0 turn23search2 |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe available community performance evidence argues against universal millisecond estimates. On one Mac setup, Omnisearch, Dataview, Git, QuickAdd and Templater were all reported in the tens of milliseconds at startup. A different cold-start report saw individual plugins in the hundreds or thousands of milliseconds. Android reports have identified Projects, Spaced Repetition, Natural Language Dates, Tasks and Calendar as meaningful startup contributors in specific configurations; another report associated Omnisearch\'s initial index with crashes around a few thousand notes. These are not controlled benchmarks, but together they show that **configuration, platform, cache state and vault content dominate simple plugin reputation**. cite turn15search16 turn15search13 turn15search2 turn15search11\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"2eb301","wall_time_seconds":0.3092373,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4619,"output":"There is also evidence that **very large vaults stress Obsidian core itself**, independent of community plugins. Users have reported tens-of-thousands-of-file vaults taking substantial startup/indexing time even in restricted mode, and a 14k-note import/reindex report saw long waits. This means you should not attribute every slowdown to plugins. cite turn15search0 turn15search4\\r\\n\\r\\nOn **privacy and security**, the crucial rule is that “local plugin” does not mean “sandboxed plugin.” Obsidian explicitly warns that it cannot reliably restrict community-plugin permissions; community plugins can potentially access files, connect to the internet and perform actions with Obsidian\'s privileges. Your student-record folders therefore deserve a stricter threat model than ordinary knowledge notes. cite turn8search0\\r\\n\\r\\nMy privacy hierarchy is:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Privacy tier | Examples | Assessment |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **Local, manual, narrow** | QuickAdd, Quick Switcher++, Linter, Tag Wrangler, Modal Forms | Lowest concern when configured without network scripts. |\\r\\n| **Local but highly privileged/programmatic** | Templater, DataviewJS, Meta Bind scripting, Shell Commands | Data may remain local, but user scripts can execute powerful actions. cite turn12search2 turn9search1 |\\r\\n| **Local service/API** | Local REST API | No cloud required, but the Bearer [REDACTED] effectively grants broad vault control. Keep HTTPS endpoint on loopback and do not deliberately expose the plain HTTP service to the LAN. cite turn24search0 |\\r\\n| **Local index** | Omnisearch, Smart Connections | Data remains local in normal core configuration; index size and folder exclusions matter. Smart Connections specifically states local embeddings/default-local processing. cite turn14search2 turn23search1 |\\r\\n| **Provider-dependent AI** | Claudian, Grimoire, Copilot | The integration may be locally hosted, but selected prompts/files/tool outputs can go to Anthropic/OpenAI/other providers. cite turn24search2 turn23search2 |\\r\\n| **Configured remote storage** | Git, remote-sync plugins | Whatever files you track/sync are transmitted to the remote you configure. Obsidian Git\'s remote behaviour is explicit. cite turn22search0 |\\r\\n\\r\\nFor your **15,000-note vault specifically**, my strongest large-vault architecture preferences are Quick Switcher++, QuickAdd, Templater, Linter, Tag Wrangler and Local REST API because none inherently requires a second semantic representation of all 15,000 notes. Tasks is the next safest indexed tool because the index solves a clear operational problem without creating another copy of note semantics. Notebook Navigator and Omnisearch are worth their indexes only if they measurably improve daily navigation/retrieval. Smart Connections gets the highest scrutiny because embeddings are both computationally useful and computationally nontrivial. cite turn14search0 turn24search1 turn12search2 turn17view0 turn16search0 turn24search0 turn13search1 turn14search1 turn14search2 turn23search1\\r\\n\\r\\n## Installation, safe testing, and thirty-day evaluation\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore adding anything, establish a repeatable **performance baseline in the real vault**. Do ten cold launches and record the median launch-to-responsive-editor time. After five idle minutes, record Obsidian memory and CPU three times in macOS Activity Monitor. Run the same 20 representative searches, open the same 20 representative notes, switch between five large notes, and type continuously into one large teaching note. Repeat these measurements after each major plugin group. This is intentionally simple enough to repeat; a sophisticated benchmark that you will not actually run is less valuable.\\r\\n\\r\\nA reasonable acceptance envelope for an 8 GB machine is to reject or defer a plugin if, **for its actual usefulness**, it causes one of the following in your measured configuration: roughly >10–15% persistent memory growth, sustained idle CPU that was not present before, >250 ms added median startup time without compensating value, repeated UI pauses during typing/note switching, or a large index that duplicates functionality you rarely invoke. Those are decision thresholds for your experiment, not claims about what Obsidian “should” consume.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Never test a powerful mutating plugin with its destructive/bulk features first.** Use a small set of newly created representative test notes. Do not delete or move existing files. For Linter, Tag Wrangler, Git, PDF++, agent tools and attachment-management plugins, validate read-only or narrow operations before enabling automatic/bulk behaviour.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Plugin | Real workflow test | Test period | Measurable success criterion | Expected performance effect | Clean reversal |\\r\\n|---|---|---:|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **Quick Switcher++** | Navigate to 50 curriculum/student/business/research targets by keyboard | 3 days | ≥90% of targets found without opening Explorer/search; navigation feels instantaneous | Low | Disable; no content migration |\\r\\n| **QuickAdd** | Build 3 actions: session capture, research-source capture, business decision | 5 days | ≥20 successful captures; zero misfiled notes; repeated capture takes <15 s | Low | Disable choices; created Markdown remains ordinary |\\r\\n| **Templater** | Standardize 3 existing note types | 5 days | ≥95% templates render without manual repair; clear reduction in setup steps | Low; script-dependent | Disable; rendered Markdown remains |\\r\\n| **Linter** | Enable only 5–10 low-risk formatting/YAML rules on newly edited notes | 7 days | 100 edited notes with zero unwanted semantic change | Low/edit-triggered | Disable automatic lint; leave files untouched |\\r\\n| **Tag Wrangler** | Manage several newly created test tags; do **not** mass-rename production tags initially | 7 days | Faster tag maintenance with zero unexpected replacements | Low/manual | Disable; no additional runtime state required |\\r\\n| **Local REST API** | Week one: agent **read/search/open only**; week two: targeted PATCH on dedicated test notes | 7–14 days | ≥20 correct agent operations; no wrong-file edits; server remains loopback-only | Low idle server + request spikes | Disable plugin/revoke token; Markdown unaffected |\\r\\n| **Tasks** | Track ~20 real tasks embedded where work originates | 7 days | Daily task view becomes reliable; queries remain responsive; no need for external task duplicate | Moderate index | Disable; checkboxes remain plain Markdown |\\r\\n| **Notebook Navigator** | Replace Explorer/recent-files navigation for normal work | 7 days | Used ≥20 times/day; memory delta acceptable; no mobile reloads/lag | Moderate local cache | Disable and return to Explorer |\\r\\n| **Omnisearch** | Run 30 “I know this text exists somewhere” searches | 5 days after indexing | ≥80% useful top-five results; typical result <0.5 s; post-index idle CPU near baseline | Moderate initial/index memory | Disable; search index ceases running |\\r\\n| **Modal Forms** | Student-session distillate + product-decision forms | 7 days | ≥20 submissions with complete standardized properties and fewer entry mistakes | Low/manual | Disable; generated notes stay Markdown |\\r\\n| **Meta Bind** | Build one operational dashboard over existing frontmatter | 7 days | Dashboard used repeatedly; no typing/note-open lag; removes manual YAML editing | Low–moderate per reactive note | Disable; properties remain |\\r\\n| **Excalidraw** | Create 3 genuine LSAT curriculum/research maps | 14 days | Each map reopened/reused at least twice; visual relationships improve explanation/planning | View-triggered, potentially high on complex scenes | Disable; drawings remain stored |\\r\\n| **Advanced Canvas** | One process map and one research map using native Canvas | 14 days | Advanced features are used repeatedly rather than merely decorative | View-triggered | Disable; retain standard-compatible Canvas where possible |\\r\\n| **PDF++** | Annotate research PDFs using **backlink/Markdown mode only** | 7 days | ≥20 annotations remain useful from normal notes/backlinks | View-triggered | Disable; Markdown annotations/links remain |\\r\\n| **Spaced Repetition** | Build a small LSAT concept/reasoning review set | 14 days | ≥4 review sessions/week and evidence that scheduling actually improves curriculum recall/QA | Low–moderate | Disable; cards remain Markdown |\\r\\n| **Git** | Begin with local commits/manual status; no aggressive background interval | 7 days | Version history proves useful ≥3 times; commits do not create noticeable freezes | Manual process scans; network only when remote used | Disable plugin; repository remains ordinary Git |\\r\\n| **Lazy Loader** | Install **only if measured startup is unsatisfactory** | 5 days | ≥25% median startup improvement with no unavailable commands/dependency failures | Moves startup work later | Disable and restore normal plugin load |\\r\\n| **Smart Connections** | Exclude privacy-sensitive folders; run 20 queries where exact terms are unknown | 7 days after initial index | ≥70% of queries produce at least one genuinely useful top-five result unavailable through ordinary search; memory/idle CPU stay acceptable | High initial + persistent index | Disable; no note content changes required |\\r\\n| **Claudian or Grimoire — one only** | Ten multi-step agent jobs inside a dedicated non-sensitive workspace | 7 days | ≥7/10 are faster than terminal workflow and no unexpected file mutations | High transient AI/process cost | Disable; continue agents externally |\\r\\n| **BRAT** | Beta testing only | Duration of beta test | Beta provides a specific needed fix/feature | Network/update checks | Disable once beta test ends |\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal REST API deserves an additional security gate. Its documented HTTPS endpoint is loopback `127.0.0.1`, authenticated by Bearer [REDACTED]; the plugin can optionally enable plain HTTP. For your production vault, keep the authenticated HTTPS endpoint local and do **not** enable broader network exposure simply to make a client easier to configure. cite turn24search0\\r\\n\\r\\nSmart Connections deserves a retrieval gate rather than a “this feels intelligent” gate. Its documentation explicitly explains that semantic results differ from exact-text results and that it maintains local embeddings/indexing. Therefore compare the same research questions across core search, Omnisearch, Smart Connections and your external agent search. Keep the embedding layer only if it surfaces materially different useful notes. cite turn23search1\\r\\n\\r\\nA practical thirty-day sequence is:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Period | Additions | Decision being tested |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **Days 1–3** | No new plugins; measure baseline | Establish startup, idle RAM/CPU, retrieval and UI latency |\\r\\n| **Days 4–7** | Quick Switcher++, QuickAdd, Templater, Linter, Tag Wrangler | Does the low-cost productivity layer produce immediate repeated value? |\\r\\n| **Days 8–11** | Local REST API with read-only agent workflows first | Does a direct Obsidian-agent control plane outperform ad hoc filesystem access? |\\r\\n| **Days 12–15** | Tasks + Modal Forms | Can task execution and structured business/student capture become deterministic? |\\r\\n| **Days 16–19** | Notebook Navigator | Does visual navigation beat Explorer enough to justify its cache/UI layer? |\\r\\n| **Days 20–22** | Omnisearch | Is an extra full-text index materially better than existing retrieval? |\\r\\n| **Days 23–25** | One specialized knowledge tool: Excalidraw, PDF++, or Spaced Repetition | Install according to the workflow you actually perform most |\\r\\n| **Days 26–27** | Advanced Canvas/Meta Bind only if a demonstrated use case remains | Prevent speculative dashboard/visual complexity |\\r\\n| **Days 28–29** | One AI experiment: Smart Connections **or** Claudian/Grimoire | Semantic retrieval vs embedded-agent UI; do not add several at once |\\r\\n| **Day 30** | Remove from the enabled set anything without repeated value; measure final baseline again | The final stack must outperform the initial system in both capability and responsiveness |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe installation order I ultimately recommend appears as ranking **J** at the end of this report.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Final rankings, limitations, and source register\\r\\n\\r\\nThe strongest evidence in this research comes from **primary sources**: the official Obsidian community directory/catalogue; official Obsidian documentation for plugin security, Bases, Canvas, CLI, URI and Web Clipper; and each plugin\'s current listing/repository-linked documentation. cite turn0search0 turn0search1 turn8search0 turn10search7 turn10search10 turn8search16 turn6search0 turn8search14\\r\\n\\r\\nPrimary plugin sources included QuickAdd, Local REST API with MCP, Linter, Tag Wrangler, Meta Bind, Excalidraw, Advanced Canvas, Modal Forms, Note Toolbar, Lazy Loader, PDF++, Consistent Attachments and Links, Folder notes, Git, BRAT, Smart Connections, Grimoire, Claudian, Copilot, Claude Code IDE, Spaced Repetition, Decks, Text Extractor, Zotero Integration, tldraw and ExcaliBrain. cite turn24search1 turn24search0 turn17view0 turn16search0 turn25view1 turn25view0 turn19search0 turn18search2 turn19search1 turn19search2 turn18search1 turn18search0 turn21search0 turn22search0 turn21search2 turn23search1 turn23search2 turn24search2 turn23search5 turn24search3 turn20search1 turn20search3 turn20search2 turn18search5 turn16search10 turn16search9\\r\\n\\r\\nAdditional candidate/competitive sources included Tasks, TaskNotes, Project Manager, legacy Kanban and newer Kanban experiments; Dataview and Datacore; Omnisearch, Quick Switcher++, Notebook Navigator and Various Complements; Shell Commands and newer REST/agent tools; Zotero Sync/ZotFlow; and multiple folder-navigation alternatives. cite turn13search1 turn13search0 turn13search6 turn13search3 turn12search1 turn12search3 turn14search2 turn14search0 turn14search1 turn14search3 turn9search1 turn9search0 turn18search4 turn18search8 turn21search1 turn21search6\\r\\n\\r\\nPerformance evidence was intentionally treated as lower-confidence because most of what exists publicly is user measurement rather than controlled benchmarking. The evidence base included Mac and Windows startup-time reports, Android startup/indexing reports, large-vault indexing experiences, a 50k-note report, a 20k-note retrieval report and Lazy Loader\'s author/user benchmark. cite turn15search16 turn15search13 turn15search2 turn15search11 turn15search12 turn15search8 turn15search15\\r\\n\\r\\nThe greatest unresolved evidence gaps are **normalized memory consumption, normalized CPU at idle, consistent 15k-note startup benchmarks, and systematic security audits across releases**. Plugin listings frequently reveal architecture but do not publish reproducible resource benchmarks. Therefore every performance designation in this report should be understood as either measured anecdote or architectural inference unless explicitly described otherwise.\\r\\n\\r\\nThere is also no defensible way to equate cumulative downloads with currently active users. High-download abandoned products such as legacy Kanban and Zotero Integration illustrate the problem directly. cite turn13search3 turn18search5 turn0search9\\r\\n\\r\\n**A. Twenty highest-scoring plugins overall**\\r\\n\\r\\n| Rank | Plugin | Score | Verdict |\\r\\n|---:|---|---:|---|\\r\\n| 1 | **QuickAdd** | **95** | TRANSFORMATIVE |\\r\\n| 2 | **Local REST API with MCP** | **94** | TRANSFORMATIVE |\\r\\n| 3 | **Templater** | **93** | TRANSFORMATIVE |\\r\\n| 4 | **Quick Switcher++** | **92** | TRANSFORMATIVE |\\r\\n| 5 | **Notebook Navigator** | **90** | HIGH-IMPACT |\\r\\n| 6 | **Linter** | **89** | HIGH-IMPACT |\\r\\n| 7 | **Tag Wrangler** | **88** | HIGH-IMPACT |\\r\\n| 8 | **Tasks** | **88** | HIGH-IMPACT |\\r\\n| 9 | **Excalidraw** | **88** | HIGH-IMPACT |\\r\\n| 10 | **TaskNotes** | **87** | HIGH-IMPACT, but not alongside Tasks by default |\\r\\n| 11 | **Omnisearch** | **86** | HIGH-IMPACT |\\r\\n| 12 | **Advanced Canvas** | **85** | STRONG |\\r\\n| 13 | **Modal Forms** | **85** | STRONG |\\r\\n| 14 | **Meta Bind** | **84** | STRONG |\\r\\n| 15 | **Lazy Loader** | **83** | STRONG, conditional |\\r\\n| 16 | **Spaced Repetition** | **82** | STRONG |\\r\\n| 17 | **Claude Code IDE** | **81** | STRONG |\\r\\n| 18 | **Smart Connections** | **80** | STRONG, experimental for 8GB |\\r\\n| 19 | **Decks** | **79** | STRONG / younger |\\r\\n| 20 | **Project Manager** | **79** | STRONG / younger |\\r\\n\\r\\nThese rankings synthesize the current listing and architecture evidence summarized above. cite turn24search1 turn24search0 turn12search2 turn14search0 turn14search1 turn17view0 turn16search0 turn13search1 turn25view0 turn13search0 turn14search2\\r\\n\\r\\n**B. Ten plugins most likely to transform daily work**\\r\\n\\r\\n| Rank | Plugin | Transformation |\\r\\n|---:|---|---|\\r\\n| 1 | **QuickAdd** | Converts repetitive capture/workflow sequences into single actions |\\r\\n| 2 | **Local REST API with MCP** | Turns Obsidian into a live tool/API for Claude/Codex workflows |\\r\\n| 3 | **Quick Switcher++** | Removes navigation friction across 15k notes |\\r\\n| 4 | **Templater** | Makes structured note creation deterministic |\\r\\n| 5 | **Notebook Navigator** | Can replace several browsing/recent/calendar UI plugins |\\r\\n| 6 | **Tasks** | Makes operational work queryable without leaving Markdown |\\r\\n| 7 | **Linter** | Keeps human/AI-generated Markdown structurally consistent |\\r\\n| 8 | **Omnisearch** | Makes large-vault text retrieval practical when core search falls short |\\r\\n| 9 | **Modal Forms** | Converts inconsistent manual record entry into structured capture |\\r\\n| 10 | **Excalidraw** | Adds a genuinely different visual cognition surface rather than another text utility |\\r\\n\\r\\nEvidence: current plugin listings and capabilities cited throughout the scorecard. cite turn24search1 turn24search0 turn14search0 turn12search2 turn14search1 turn13search1 turn17view0 turn14search2 turn18search2 turn25view0\\r\\n\\r\\n**C. Ten strongest large-vault plugins**\\r\\n\\r\\n| Rank | Plugin | Large-vault rationale |\\r\\n|---:|---|---|\\r\\n| 1 | **Quick Switcher++** | High navigation leverage without semantic index |\\r\\n| 2 | **QuickAdd** | Mostly targeted/manual work |\\r\\n| 3 | **Linter** | Incremental hygiene on changed notes |\\r\\n| 4 | **Tag Wrangler** | High-value ontology maintenance |\\r\\n| 5 | **Local REST API with MCP** | Agent access without creating a second vault index |\\r\\n| 6 | **Templater** | Targeted note-generation work |\\r\\n| 7 | **Tasks** | Purpose-specific index with no per-task-file requirement |\\r\\n| 8 | **Lazy Loader** | Can mitigate plugin-startup bottlenecks after measurement |\\r\\n| 9 | **Notebook Navigator** | Potentially excellent; conditional on local cache benchmark |\\r\\n"}'}]
